# P3: Hooks (Claude and Codex) implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Status:** revision 3, after Sol rounds 1 and 2 (see "Review log" at the end).
Gates 2, 4, 5 and 7 are confirmed (commit a6e08dd).

**Goal:** Make Claude Code and Codex sessions first-class in acd. Claude
`http` hooks and the `acd hook codex` shim feed a hook endpoint in acd.
Claude permission prompts are held and answered through the hook reply.
Questions, Codex approvals, and Claude prompts whose hook has ended are
answered with guarded keys through P2, but only when the dialog on
screen provably shows the hook's exact command or question. Sessions are
bound to herdr panes, migrate when a binding appears, and lose every
request, hold, target and pending read when T3 or another owner takes
them. `acd hooks install|uninstall`, hook-token rotation and `acd
service install` ship with it, plus every P3 obligation from the P1
follow-ups.

**Architecture:** A new `hooks` module in `crates/acd-adapters`. An axum
router (`hooks/endpoint.rs`) behind a connection-capped listener
authenticates `POST /hook/<agent>/<event>`, tags each request with the
credential generation it matched, replies to lifecycle hooks at once,
and parks Claude `PermissionRequest` connections as **holds**. Lifecycle
events go into a bounded, byte-budgeted mailbox; holds, closes and
expiries go into a separate control channel that is never dropped. One
**hooks reconciler task** (`hooks/state.rs`) is the only code that
upserts hooks-only agents and opens, migrates, revokes or ends hook
requests. It never awaits herdr: screen reads run in spawned,
semaphore-bounded tasks carrying a **read ticket** (pane incarnation,
binding generation, occurrence generation, read sequence), and their
results are revalidated inside the per-agent delivery lock before any
observation or target registration. `HooksAdapter` implements
`acd_core::Adapter` for `AnswerPath::Hook` and `Source::Hooks`. Keys
answers stay in P2's engine, which now takes the delivery lock before its
guard read and checks the pane incarnation in its lease. The `acd`
binary gains a transactional hook-token store, the shim, a serialized
and journaled installer, rotation, the service installer, and the
listener wiring. Core gains agent migration, a pending-key lookup and a
resolution `reason`.

**Tech Stack:** Rust (edition 2024, rust-version 1.90), tokio, axum 0.8
(hook listener, now a normal dependency of `acd-adapters`), serde_json
with the `raw_value` feature (order-preserving settings edits), sha2,
chrono (registry `procStart` parsing), libc (process start time,
`flock`), acd-securefs (no-follow reads). The shim uses only `std::net`.
Tests use FakeHerdr with gated reads, the core `testing` feature, a
raw-TCP hook client, debug-only failure injection, and temp `HOME`
directories.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-agent-inbox-design.md`:
§4 (invariants 1 to 4), §5 and §5.1 (keys, occurrences, attribution,
upgrade, exclusive answer paths, agent rules), §6 (adapter contract),
§6.1 (endpoint, installation, token storage, rotation, coexisting
hooks), §6.2 (Claude), §6.3 (Codex), §6.4 (herdr fallback, follow-ups),
§7.1 (tokens, logs), §9 (config and commands), §11 gates 2, 4, 5 and 7,
§12, §13 and §14 items 1, 2, 4, 6, 8 and 9. Evidence:
`docs/research/2026-10-07-p0-checks.md`,
`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gates-claude.md` (gates 2, 5, 7),
`docs/research/2026-10-09-p3-gate4-codex.md` (gate 4), and the fixtures
in `tests/fixtures/hooks/{claude,codex}/` and `tests/fixtures/herdr/`.
Obligations: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-p1-followups.md`,
section "P3: hooks and service" (mapped one by one below). Reviews: Sol
round 1 (`/tmp/acp3/review/sol-r1.md`), recorded in the Review log.

## Gate results (confirmed in a6e08dd)

Each item names the result, where the plan depends on it, and the task
that carries a `GATE <n>:` note at that point.

1. **GATE 2: header env interpolation (2a).** `$VAR` in a header value is
   replaced when listed in `allowedEnvVars`; an unlisted variable becomes
   `""`. Hook config is a snapshot taken at session start, re-read only
   on `/clear`, resume or restart. Plan: Claude entries carry
   `X-Herdr-Pane: $HERDR_PANE_ID`, `X-Herdr-Socket: $HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`,
   `X-Claude-Entrypoint: $CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` and the literal token
   (Task 23); an empty header is absent (Task 8).
2. **GATE 2: held connection on a terminal answer (2b).** It closes about
   15 ms after a terminal deny or Esc and stays open on a terminal allow
   until the hook timeout. Plan: a close means deny, Esc, interruption or
   death (Task 15); a terminal allow is detected from `PostToolUse` for
   an undelivered request or from the matched dialog leaving the screen
   (Tasks 14, 15).
3. **GATE 2: another hook decides; late replies (2c, 2d).** Claude does
   not wait for the held hook and ignores a later reply; a denial leaves
   `Stop` without `PostToolUse`. Plan: attribution in Task 14.
4. **GATE 2: `allow_always` (2e).** `addRules` with `localSettings`
   persists across sessions; Bash requests carry no `addRules`
   suggestion. Plan (ruling Q2): acd builds the rule from the exact
   command (Tasks 7, 14).
5. **GATE 2: stalled endpoint (2f).** Lifecycle hooks proceed after
   1.0 s. Plan: lifecycle timeout 1 s (Task 23).
6. **GATE 2: no http `SessionStart`.** Claude never sends it over http.
   Plan: not installed; the registry scan and any later hook create the
   agent (Tasks 9, 11, 23).
7. **GATE 4: Codex binding.** The `HERDR_*` env of Codex hooks is the
   app-server's and is stale or names another pane; herdr's Codex
   `agent_session` matches the hook `session_id` when present, is absent
   in shared-daemon mode, and can be attached to the wrong pane. Plan
   (ruling Q3 and round-2 ruling B4): env never used; bind by exactly one
   pane reporting the session, else by the only Codex pane in the cwd
   across all panes; any competing pane showing the same command makes
   it read-only (Tasks 10, 17).
8. **GATE 5: session registry.** Statuses `idle`, `busy`, `waiting`
   (+ `waitingFor`); `procStart` is `ps -o lstart=` in UTC; a `kill -9`
   leaves the entry; broken files fail to parse or open. Plan: Task 9.
9. **GATE 7: hook token rotation.** Running sessions keep their
   launch-time token; a rejected token is non-blocking but noisy. Plan
   (ruling Q4 and round-2 rulings I1, I2): a transactional token store
   with credential generations; the previous token stays valid until no
   live session started before the headers were updated remains unseen
   on the new generation, and never past 30 days (Tasks 21, 26, 27, 31).
10. **LIVE CHECK: Claude tip lines.** If a `Tip:` line rotates under a
    visible dialog, the target watch would publish a false `terminal`.
    Task 32 Step 6.
11. **LIVE CHECK: Codex user-level hook trust.** Whether
    `~/.codex/hooks.json` needs a one-time review. Task 32 Step 6.
12. **LIVE CHECK: command rendering.** How Claude and Codex render a
    command that wraps or spans lines. Until measured, such commands
    never bind to a screen (Dialog match contract, M4). Task 32 Step 6.
13. **LIVE CHECK: parallel tool calls.** Whether Claude fires several
    `PermissionRequest` hooks before the first dialog ends (Occurrence
    contract, O1). Task 32 Step 6.
14. **LIVE CHECK (release gate): registry entry delay.** How long after
    a Claude process starts its registry entry appears, including
    sessions started during a rotation and under load; `REGISTRY_GRACE_MS`
    (T4) must exceed the worst observed delay. An underestimated grace
    can miss a live old-token session and retire the previous token
    early. Task 32 Step 6; P3 is not released until it passes.

- **Q4:** per-session retention with a 30-day cap.

Round 2 (on Sol round 1): every blocking, important and minor finding is

normative result.

## Amendment A1 (2026-10-09): hook transport and port squatting

Found by an automated security scan of Task 22 and confirmed by the Task
22 Opus review and a Sol opinion (/tmp/acp3/sol/a1.last.md). When acd is
not holding `127.0.0.1:<hook_port>` (stopped, crashed, restarting, or
started after the agents), another OS user, or a sandboxed process that can
bind loopback but cannot read the 0700 config dir, can bind the port,
receive `X-Acd-Token` and the payload, and answer a `PermissionRequest`
with an allow, including a persistent `allow_always`. The client protocol
already avoids this (acd.sock locally, pinned TLS remotely).

- **Codex: Unix socket only.** acd also serves the hook endpoint (same
  router, auth and bounds) on `<config_dir>/hook.sock`. The shim connects
  only to it and never falls back to TCP. Contract:
  - Before connecting, the shim checks that the config dir is not a
    symlink, is owned by the effective uid and has mode 0700, and that
    `hook.sock` is a socket (not a symlink) owned by the effective uid with
    mode 0600. Any failure, a missing socket or a refused connection fails
    open (no output, exit 0), which restores Codex's own dialog. The total
    deadline covers stdin, connect, write and response parsing.
  - acd creates `hook.sock` with permissions set before it serves. Startup
    is serialized by an exclusive daemon lock. A pre-existing path is
    replaced only on a definite stale condition (a socket owned by the
    effective uid whose connect fails with ECONNREFUSED); a live socket,
    another file type or owner, or any other error (permission, timeout,
    resource exhaustion) refuses to start. Shutdown removes the socket only
    if it is still the one this process created (same inode). The same
    rules replace the current `daemon.rs::bind_local_socket` behaviour for
    `acd.sock`.
  - Tests: concurrent starts, stale replacement, live-socket refusal,
    unsafe dir/socket owner or mode, symlinked dir or socket, cleanup not
    removing a successor's socket.
  - Task 8 adds the Unix listener to the endpoint, Task 22 gets a fix
    round (shim side), Task 30 wires the listener and the startup rules.
- **Claude: v1 uses `http` hooks on loopback to preserve the verified
  gate-2 behaviour, with the residual risk accepted by the user
  (2026-10-09) for personal, trusted-host use.** Claude also supports
  command hooks; moving Claude to the shim is a post-v1 follow-up and needs
  gate 2 re-tested for command hooks. Claude's http hooks offer no way to
  authenticate the server (no response signature check; an echoed secret,
  custom header or health preflight cannot prove identity). v1 mitigations,
  all defence in depth, not a fix:
  - `acd init` picks a random free hook port (bind port 0, record it in
    `config.toml`), so the port is not a well-known default. Existing
    configs keep their port. Running Claude sessions snapshot the URL, so
    the port never changes after install.
  - acd binds `hook_port` before reporting ready and refuses to start if
    the bind fails (D13).
  - `acd status` and `acd hooks status` warn, best effort, when the hook
    port is held by another process.
  - Spec §6.1 and §9 distinguish client authentication from server
    authentication, describe token and payload disclosure and forged or
    persistent approvals while acd is down, and state that v1 does not
    protect against hostile local users, loopback-capable sandboxes, or
    same-uid processes. The install note says so too.
- **Shim hardening (Task 22 fix round):** bound the stdin read by the
  deadline; stop reading the reply after the head plus `Content-Length`
  bytes; `catch_unwind` with a silent panic hook so the shim always exits 0
  with no stderr; `HERDR_PANE_ID` in the harness `ISOLATED` list; assert
  empty stderr in the non-2xx test.

## Contracts

These sections are normative. Tasks implement them and cite them by
item number.

### Occurrence contract (Sol question 1, finding I3)

- **O1. A Claude tool `PermissionRequest` is one new dialog
  occurrence.** Claude and Codex do not retry hooks: each hook HTTP
  request (or shim run) is one invocation, so byte-identical repeats are
  new occurrences too. Before opening it, every older open hook-path
  occurrence of the same session ends: a `Held` one (no reply written)
  resolves `terminal` and its hold is released empty; a `Delivered` one
  (reply written, awaiting evidence) resolves `uncertain` (D10); a
  `HoldEnded`, `Keys` or `ReadOnly` one resolves `terminal` and its
  target is retired. Basis: Claude shows one permission dialog at a time
  per session (P0, gate 2); a new dialog means the previous one ended.
  If parallel tool calls ever fire several hooks at once (LIVE CHECK
  13), the older hold is released empty and that dialog falls back to the
  terminal: fail open.
- **O2. `AskUserQuestion`.** A `PreToolUse` opens the occurrence. A
  `PermissionRequest` for `AskUserQuestion` with the same key while that
  occurrence is open and no `PostToolUse` arrived merges into it. One
  with no open occurrence opens one. A second `PreToolUse` with the same
  key is a new occurrence (O1 applies to the old one).
- **O3. Codex `PermissionRequest`** is one new occurrence; O1's ending
  rule applies to older open occurrences of the session.
- **O4. At most one open occurrence per session.** Because of O1 to O3,
  a session never has two open hook occurrences, so one evidence event
  can only ever apply to one request.
- **O5. Evidence** applies only to the session's open occurrence, only
  if the event arrived after the occurrence opened and, for a delivered
  answer, after the delivery time. Events older than that, events after
  the request ended, and events of other sessions do nothing.
- **O6. Phases.** `Held` (hold open, nothing written) → `Delivered`
  (`deliver` wrote the reply; the hold is consumed) or `HoldEnded`
  (closed or expired, nothing written) → ended. Separately: `Keys` (a
  target is registered; the herdr engine and watch own the outcome) and
  `ReadOnly`. `deliver` records `Delivered` in shared state under a
  `std` mutex; the reconciler reads it as the phase.
- **O7. Ambiguous identical evidence (Sol N4).** Claude sends no
  invocation id that a later `PostToolUse` repeats, and `HookEvent.at`
  is the receive time, so a delayed outcome of an older occurrence
  cannot be told apart from the outcome of a newer one with the same
  evidence identity (Claude tool: tool name and digest; Codex: tool name
  and command; question: question text). Therefore:
  - When an occurrence ends in any way without having seen identity
    evidence (`ToolRan` or `Answered`), the session keeps an
    **unconsumed identity** entry for it (its `Expect`). An entry is
    removed by the first identity-matching event of that session, by a
    turn end of that session (`Stop`, `UserPromptSubmit`; processed after
    the event's own effect on open occurrences), or after
    `unconsumed_ttl` (10 min). At most 8 entries per session: when one is
    dropped at the cap, the session's `shadow_all` flag is set until its
    next turn end or `unconsumed_ttl`.
  - Keys requests resolved by the herdr engine through the evidence hub
    also leave an entry (the reconciler does not see the hub's verdict).
    The cost is a conservative shadow until the turn ends, never a wrong
    attribution.
  - A new occurrence whose identity equals an unconsumed entry of its
    session (`evidence::same_identity`: tool name and digest, tool name
    and command, or question text; option labels are not part of a
    question's identity) or that opens while `shadow_all` is set is
    **shadowed** for its whole life. Question identity evidence exists
    only when the answers map contains the expected question with a
    string answer; any other `AskUserQuestion` outcome is no evidence
    (Sol R3-2).
  - For a shadowed occurrence, identity-matching evidence is ignored:
    it neither resolves it (`terminal` for an undelivered request) nor
    confirms or contradicts a delivered answer, in `apply_evidence` and
    in the keys `EvidenceHub` alike. It is consumed against the oldest
    unconsumed entry instead. A turn end still ends a shadowed request,
    but only as `terminal` when acd delivered nothing; after a delivered
    answer (hook reply or keys) it resolves `uncertain`, because the
    ignored identity evidence could have confirmed or contradicted it. A
    shadowed request therefore ends only through its own signals: its
    hold (reply, close, expiry), its screen reads (presence, hold-end), a
    turn end, a newer occurrence (O1, O3) or the evidence window
    (`uncertain` for a delivered answer, as D10).

### Dialog match contract (finding B1)

A hook binds to an on-screen dialog only through these rules. Anything
else leaves the request without a screen match: read-only, or for a held
Claude request, answerable only through its hold (spec §6.2 step 6).
The rules work on the raw rows of the dialog region that
`screen::find_dialog` located (exposed as `Dialog::region` and
`Dialog::option_rows`, Task 13), never on `Dialog::text` or
`Dialog::identity`.

- **M1. Claude Bash permission.** The dialog kind is `ClaudePermission`.
  The first non-blank row after the top rule, trimmed, is exactly
  `Bash command`. The region has exactly two dashed rule rows (`╌`), both
  above the first option row. Between them there is exactly one row. That
  row, after removing exactly one leading space (the dialog margin) and
  its trailing whitespace, equals the hook's `tool_input.command`
  byte for byte. Other Claude tools never match.
- **M2. Codex permission.** The dialog kind is `CodexPermission`. Among
  the rows between the region's first row and the first option row,
  exactly one row starts with two spaces followed by `$ `. The next row is
  blank or is the first option row. That row, after removing the leading
  `  $ ` and trailing whitespace, equals the hook's `tool_input.command`
  byte for byte. A collapsed dialog never matches (it is only reported as
  collapsed).
- **M3. Claude question.** The dialog kind is `ClaudeQuestion { tabs:
  false }`. The row containing `☐`, trimmed, is exactly `☐ <header>`.
  Between that row and the first option row there is exactly one
  non-blank row, and it equals the question text after trimming. The
  options block numbers 1 to n+2 in order; labels 1 to n equal the hook's
  option labels exactly; label n+1 is `Type something.` and label n+2 is
  `Chat about this`.
- **M4. Wrapping and whitespace.** herdr's screen text carries no
  soft-wrap marks, and herdr reports no pane width (`pane.list` has only
  `viewport_rows`), so a row break cannot be told apart from a newline,
  a dropped space at a word wrap, or a hard wrap inside a word. Therefore
  "terminal line wrapping" is defined as any rendering of the command or
  question that spans more than one row, and such a rendering never
  matches: no screen binding and no keys target (D26). A held Claude
  request is still answerable through its HTTP hold. Inside the single
  row, whitespace is compared exactly, including runs of spaces inside
  quotes. A hook command that contains
  `\n`, `\t`, `\r` or trailing whitespace never matches. The orchestrator
  asked for wrapped commands to match under a precise definition; this is
  narrowed with evidence in the Review log (finding B1) and LIVE CHECK 12
  measures real wrapping for a later, separately reviewed extension.
- **M5. Uniqueness.** A match also requires that the region parse is
  unambiguous: exactly the counts above. A forged description or reason
  that adds rule rows, `$ ` rows or extra question rows makes the count
  wrong, so nothing matches.

### Read tickets and generations (finding B2)

- **G1. Pane incarnation.** Every `PaneRef` carries `incarnation: u64`,
  drawn from a process-wide counter whenever the herdr reconciler adds a
  pane as a new agent (first sight, or replacement after a close or an
  identity change, Task 5). Targets bind to it (Task 6).
- **G2. Binding generation.** Each Claude session and each Codex session
  has `bind_gen: u64`, bumped on every owner or binding change and every
  revocation (Ownership transitions).
- **G3. Occurrence generation.** Each track has `op: u64` from a
  reconciler-wide counter. Reads have a `Purpose` (`Capture`,
  `Presence`, `HoldEnded`; Codex tracks use `CodexBind` instead of
  `Capture`). Each track keeps, per purpose, `read_seq: u64` and at most
  one outstanding read (Sol N3). `read_seq` is bumped only when a read
  is admitted (A3), never for a read that was not started.
- **G4. Ticket.** Every spawned read carries `Ticket { agent_id,
  incarnation, session_id, bind_gen, request_id, op, purpose, read_seq }`.
  A Codex binding watch, which runs before a request exists, uses its
  session's `bind_gen`, `op = 0` and the session's own watch sequence.
- **G5. Revalidation.** A read result is applied only inside the
  delivery lock of `ticket.agent_id`, after rechecking: the pane is still
  in the pane map with the same incarnation; the session's `bind_gen` is
  unchanged; T3 does not own the session; for track reads, the track
  exists with the same `op` and `read_seq` is the track's latest for the
  ticket's purpose; the request is still open on the expected answer
  path. Otherwise the result is dropped. Per-purpose latest-wins
  `read_seq` handles out-of-order completion, and a read of one purpose
  never invalidates a read of another.
- **G6. Bounded reads.** Admission and cancellation follow A3 and A4.

### Ownership transitions (finding B3)

Owners: `Hooks` (hooks-only agent `claude:<sid>`), `Herdr(a)` (bound to
herdr agent `a`), `T3(t)`, `Unknown`. For Codex: `Bound(a)`,
`T3(t)`, `Unbound`. Every transition bumps `bind_gen`. "Revoke" means:
for each track of the session, inside the delivery lock of the track's
agent, retire its keys target, end the request (`uncertain` if it is
`answering`, otherwise `lost`), observe `DialogGoneById`, release its
hold empty and drop its expectation. Revocation never removes a herdr
agent.

| From → to | Action |
|---|---|
| same owner | nothing |
| `Hooks` → `Herdr(b)` | migrate `claude:<sid>` into `b` (Task 2): the request keeps its id; tracks re-point to `b` and restart capture |
| `Hooks` → `T3`/`Unknown` | revoke, then `AgentGone(claude:<sid>)` |
| `Herdr(a)` → `T3`/`Unknown` | revoke on `a`; `a` stays |
| `Herdr(a)` → `Hooks` | revoke on `a`; upsert `claude:<sid>`; requests are not carried back (the next hook reopens) |
| `Herdr(a)` → `Herdr(b)`, a ≠ b | revoke on `a`; new requests open on `b` |
| `T3`/`Unknown` → `Hooks` | upsert `claude:<sid>` |
| `T3`/`Unknown` → `Herdr(b)` | record the binding |
| `T3` ↔ `Unknown` | nothing |
| Codex `Bound(a)` → `T3`/`Unbound`/`Bound(b)` | revoke on `a` |
| Codex `T3`/`Unbound` → `Bound(b)` | record the binding |

A pane replaced under the same agent id (new incarnation) is handled by
herdr (its agent is removed and re-added, which resolves the request
`lost`), and every outstanding ticket for the old incarnation fails G5.

The Codex rows are evaluated for every Codex session with an open track
on every tick, on every herdr pane-map change and on every T3 change:
`codex_bind` is recomputed against the current panes and compared with
the track's pane (Sol N1). `Bound(a)` means the track's pane is `a` and
validation C2 still holds for it; anything else is a transition.

### Codex binding validation (finding N1)

- **C1. One path.** A Codex request is enabled (gets a keys target, or
  its "enlarge the pane" note is dropped) only through
  `validate_codex` inside the delivery lock of the bound pane, both for
  the first watch and for every later enabling read (a collapsed dialog
  that later shows in full). No other code registers a Codex target.
- **C2. Inputs.** A Codex bind read reads the bound pane and then every
  other Codex pane in the pane map at read time (its rivals), and
  returns per pane: `Shows` (M2 matches), `NotShown` (read succeeded,
  not truncated, no match) or `Unknown` (read failed, timed out or
  was truncated). It records the rival inventory it read:
  `(agent_id, incarnation)` for each rival.
- **C3. Decision.** Inside the lock, the request is enabled only if: the
  bound pane `Shows`; every rival is `NotShown`; the current rival
  inventory (every Codex pane but the bound one, now) equals the
  recorded inventory; `codex_bind` still picks the same pane with the
  same incarnation; and G5 holds. A rival that is `Unknown` or `Shows`,
  or a changed inventory, leaves the request read-only (an existing
  request keeps no target). An inventory change schedules one new bind
  read (A3), never a partial reuse of the old result.
- **C4. Collapsed.** A collapsed bound pane opens the request read-only
  with the note; enlarging is detected by the same bind read and goes
  through C3.
- **C5. Transitions.** Codex `Bound(a)` → `Unbound`, `Bound(b)` or `T3`
  revokes on `a` (Ownership transitions); a new rival that `Shows` the
  command while a target is live also revokes, because the binding is
  no longer unambiguous. Revocation retires the target inside the lock.
- **C6. Suspension (Sol R3-1).** A live Codex target carries a
  *validated inventory*, kept by `KeysTargets` next to the immutable
  target (`set_rivals`). The keys engine's final lease, right before the
  write and under the delivery lock, recomputes the current inventory
  from the pane map and refuses (`NotDelivered(RequestNotReady)`, zero
  keys) when it differs. This covers the interval before the reconciler
  notices. When the reconciler notices (`codex_transitions`), it takes
  the delivery lock and marks the request not ready, then schedules the
  recheck; only a C3 `Enable` on that recheck stores the new validated
  inventory and marks the request ready again. Any other outcome
  revokes. The target itself is never replaced (D8).

### Admission and service bounds (finding N2)

- **A1. Holds.** The endpoint admits a held request only with one of
  `max_holds` (64) permits. The permit travels inside `Inbound::Held`
  and is released by the reconciler when it is done with that hold: when
  it writes the reply, or when it processes the hold's `HoldClosed` or
  `HoldExpired`, or when it drops a `Held` it cannot use. A disconnected
  client therefore keeps its permit until its queued messages are
  consumed. A request with no permit fails open at once (unheld path).
- **A2. Control channel.** Each permit yields at most two control
  messages (`Held`, then one `HoldClosed` or `HoldExpired`), so the
  control channel is a bounded channel of `2 × max_holds`, and queued
  control payload bytes are at most `max_holds × max_body`.
- **A3. Reads.** A read is spawned only after `try_acquire_owned` on the
  `max_reads` (8) semaphore succeeds; it never waits for a permit. A
  read that finds no permit is recorded as wanted (one flag per track
  and purpose, one per Codex session for its bind watch) and retried on
  the next tick. The permit travels with the result and is released when
  the reconciler has processed it, so at most `max_reads` results are
  ever queued (the internal channel is bounded at `max_reads + 2`; the
  `+ 2` is one scan result and one spare).
- **A4. Cancellation.** Every spawned read keeps its `AbortHandle` in the
  track (per purpose) or Codex session. Ending a track, revoking a
  session, a newer read of the same purpose and a newer Codex event of
  the session abort the older task, which releases its permit.
- **A5. Tracks and watches.** At most `max_tracks` (256) open tracks,
  and at most `max_tracks` Codex sessions with a bind watch running or
  wanted. An occurrence over either cap opens no track: a held request is
  released empty at once (the terminal answers it), and an unheld one
  opens nothing (herdr's fallback still shows the dialog). Other
  per-session state is a few fields, expires after `idle_expiry`, and is
  created only by authenticated hooks.
- **A6. Fair service.** The reconciler loop handles, per turn: the due
  tick (timers, wanted reads, sweeps, Codex transitions), any T3 change,
  then up to `batch` (32) control messages, up to 32 internal results and
  up to 32 lifecycle events; it blocks only when all are empty. No queue
  can starve the tick or an ownership change: both are served at the
  start of every turn, and a turn handles at most 96 items.
- **A7. Lock holding (L1).** Every holder of a delivery lock releases it
  within two herdr call deadlines (`2 × CALL_TIMEOUT`, 6 s): the keys
  engine holds it around one guard read and one key write, the
  reconciler around revalidation, observation and target registration
  (no herdr call). The reconciler waits for a delivery lock without a
  timeout when it changes state (a half-applied ending or revocation
  would break O4 and the transitions table), and with `lock_wait` (2 s)
  when it applies a read result (on timeout the result is dropped and its
  purpose marked wanted). The 6 s is a bound per holder and per wait,
  not per reconciler turn: a turn that waits on several delivery locks
  can wait up to 6 s on each. Without a stalled lock, tick and ownership
  latency is one turn. Task 19 pins L1 with the real keys-engine holder
  (Sol R3-5).
- **A8. Session and history storage (Sol R3-3).** At most
  `max_sessions` (4096) Claude sessions, 4096 Codex sessions and 4096
  sessions with unconsumed identities (O7); each credential generation in
  `by_generation` holds at most `max_generation_ids` (4096) session ids,
  and at most 4 generations are kept. Session ids are at most 128 bytes
  (`valid_id`), stored `cwd` at most 4096 bytes (longer is not stored),
  names and messages are capped as in the Global Constraints, so session
  storage is bounded in entries and bytes (about 4096 × 5 KiB per map,
  4 × 4096 × 128 B of history). Eviction is conservative:
  - a full session map evicts the least recently active session with no
    open track or watch (a hooks-only one also gets `AgentGone`); if none
    can be evicted, the new session is refused (its event is dropped and
    a hold is released empty) and counted;
  - a full generation set drops further observations and counts them:
    a lost "seen on the new generation" observation only keeps the
    previous token longer (T4), never shorter;
  - evicting a session with unconsumed identities sets a global
    `shadow_all` for `unconsumed_ttl`, so every new occurrence is
    shadowed rather than an ambiguity being forgotten.

### Hook token store (findings I1, I2)

- **T1.** `<config_dir>/hook-tokens.json` (0600, written with
  `write_atomic`) is the single source of truth and the transaction:
  `{version, generation, current, previous: {token, generation,
  rotated_at_ms, expires_at_ms} | null, headers: {generation, state:
  "pending" | "done" | "failed", updated_at_ms, failed: [path]}}`.
  `headers.updated_at_ms` is the wall-clock time taken after the last
  header write finished (Sol N5), never a time captured earlier.
  `hook.token` is migrated into it by `acd init` and `acd run` and then
  removed. Every read-modify-write of the store (creation, migration,
  rotation, header state, retirement) runs under the writer lock
  `<config_dir>/hooks.lock`; plain reads take no lock, since every write
  is an atomic rename. The file is read and written through the
  structured-file helpers (F1).
- **T6. Lock discipline (Sol N7).** `writer_lock::acquire(dir)` is
  called only by top-level entry points: `hook_token::{migrate, ensure,
  update_headers, rotate, retire_if_done}` (and Task 21's interim
  `rotate_store`, deleted in Task 27) and
  `hooks_install::{install_entries, uninstall, status}`. Each holds
  the lock for its whole read-modify-write and never calls another entry
  point while holding it. Every helper that needs the lock takes
  `&WriterLock` as a parameter (`*_locked` functions) and never acquires
  it. `hook_token::load` is read-only: it never migrates and never
  locks. No path reacquires `hooks.lock`.
- **T2.** Auth matches the current and previous credentials in constant
  time and tags each request with the matched `generation`.
- **T3.** Rotation first refuses, without writing anything, when the
  current generation's headers are not `done` (repair with `acd hooks
  install claude`), when a previous token is still retained (unless
  `--force`, which cuts off the sessions still on it and says so), or
  when any recorded destination fails the precheck. The precheck reads
  each destination with the same safe reader the writes use (no
  symlink, owned by the user, a regular file) and then applies the Git
  and marker checks (Sol N8). A refusal leaves the store byte-identical.
  It then commits the new store (generation + 1, previous
  with `expires_at_ms = rotated_at_ms + 30 days`, headers `pending`),
  then rewrites managed headers idempotently to the current token, then
  records `done` or `failed`. A crash at any point leaves a store that
  authenticates both tokens; `acd hooks install claude` repairs the
  headers.
- **T4.** Retention: the previous token is retired when either
  `now ≥ expires_at_ms` (always honored), or all of: headers `done` for
  the current generation; a complete registry scan that **started** at
  least `registry_grace` (5 s, the time Claude takes to write its
  registry entry at startup) after `headers.updated_at_ms` and finished
  within the last two scan intervals, both measured after the writer
  lock is held (Sol N5); and no live session in that scan has
  `started_at < headers.updated_at_ms` unless it was observed
  authenticating with the current generation since the rotation.
  Sessions seen only on older generations are not excluded.
- **T5.** Retirement carries the whole store it evaluated (the
  `on_current` set was computed for that store) and does nothing if the
  store on disk differs in any field. It writes the store; a write failure is reported (log
  and hooks source status) and the previous token stays accepted. On
  success the in-memory auth is updated at once.

### Installer journal (finding N6)

- **J1.** `journal::begin` refuses to overwrite an existing journal. A
  journal exists only between `begin` and the record save; a failure
  before the settings rename removes it (nothing changed), a failure
  after the rename keeps it.
- **J2.** `journal::recover` runs at the start of every installer entry
  point, under the writer lock. Settings in the `after` state: apply
  `record_after`. In the `before` state: nothing changed; finish.
  Otherwise (someone, typically Claude's own atomic rewrite, changed the
  file): read it with the safe reader and classify its entries. If it
  holds owned entries for the journal's target, apply `record_after`
  when it is `Some`, else a reconstructed record (`created_file`,
  `created_hooks_key` false, `created_events` empty, so uninstall removes
  only acd's entries). If it holds none, remove the destination's
  record. Then finish.
- **J3.** If the file cannot be read safely (symlink, foreign owner, not
  a regular file, invalid JSON), the journal is kept and marked
  `conflict` with the reason. Every installer entry point, and
  `acd token rotate hook`, refuses while a conflict exists, naming the
  file and the fix; `acd hooks status` reports it. The next entry point
  after the file is fixed recovers by J2.
- **J4.** A multi-destination batch (`rewrite_claude_token`) stops at the
  first failure that leaves a journal (a record-save failure). Failures
  before the rename are per-file and the batch continues.

### Private structured files (finding N9)

- **F1.** `hook-tokens.json`, `hooks-installed.json` and
  `hooks-journal.json` are read with `secrets::read_structured` and
  written with `secrets::write_structured`: 0600, no symlink, owned by
  the user, at most `STRUCTURED_MAX` (256 KiB). The bound is checked
  before writing, so acd never writes a file it cannot read back.
  `read_secret` keeps its 4 KiB bound for single tokens only.

## Global Constraints

These apply to every task.

- **Workspace rules.** Rust edition 2024, `rust-version = "1.90"`. CI
  runs `cargo test --workspace --locked` and
  `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` on
  macOS and Linux, plus `npm run verify` in `app/`. Every task ends with
  both cargo commands green; tasks that touch protocol types also run
  `cd app && npm run verify`. A task that adds a dependency commits
  `Cargo.lock`.
- **Never touch the developer's real environment in tests.** No test
  may read or write the real `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`,
  `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, `~/.config/systemd`, `~/.config/acd`, the
  real herdr socket, or the desktop T3. Every test that runs the `acd`
  binary sets `HOME` and `ACD_CONFIG_DIR` to a temp dir and removes
  `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`,
  `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `RUST_LOG` and every `ACD_TEST_*` override.
  Library tests pass paths explicitly. No test calls `launchctl` or
  `systemctl`; tests that need the service runner override are
  `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`, because the override exists only in debug
  builds. Git, when a test needs it, runs with `HOME` set to the temp
  dir, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`.
- **Test-only overrides** (`ACD_TEST_*`) are read only under
  `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`.
- **Ports in tests.** No fixed ports. Daemon tests pick free ports for
  `port` and `hook_port`, retry the whole start on a bind failure, and
  treat the daemon as ready only after an authenticated `acd status`
  succeeds (Task 1).
- **Hook endpoint** (spec §6.1, §7.1):
  - Binds `127.0.0.1:<hook_port>` only (default 47801).
  - At most 128 open connections (the listener waits beyond that).
  - `POST /hook/{agent}/{event}` with `agent` in `claude|codex`; every
    other method, path or agent gets 401; the query string is ignored.
  - `X-Acd-Token` is compared in constant time against the current and
    previous credentials (T2). An empty stored token never matches.
  - Body at most 1 MiB (413), read within 5 s. A non-object body gets
    `200` with an empty body.
  - Lifecycle events: a bounded queue (512 events, 16 MiB of bodies).
    When full, the hook gets an empty reply and the event is dropped and
    counted (fail open). Holds, closes and expiries use a separate
    control channel bounded by the 64-hold cap; they are never dropped.
  - Header hints are accepted only when non-empty, at most 1024 bytes,
    free of control characters. `X-Herdr-Socket` is a lookup key only:
    acd never opens a path taken from a header.
  - Payload ids (`session_id`, `prompt_id`, `turn_id`, pane ids) are 1 to
    128 bytes without control characters.
- **Fail open** (invariant 1). Lifecycle hooks get their reply before
  processing. Only a Claude tool `PermissionRequest` (not
  `AskUserQuestion`) is ever held, and only for ownership `Hooks` or
  `Herdr`. Every other hook, every T3-owned, unknown or subagent session,
  every hold beyond 64 and every event dropped by a full queue gets an
  empty reply at once. A hold is released empty as soon as acd knows its
  request ended, was superseded (O1), migrated away or revoked.
- **One state owner.** Only the hooks reconciler changes hook state. Its
  loop follows A6 (from Task 19; Task 11's first loop is a biased
  `select!` that Task 19 replaces): every turn serves the tick and T3
  changes first, then bounded batches of control messages, internal
  results and lifecycle events, so no queue starves closes, timers or
  takeovers.
- **Screen-derived actions** follow the Dialog match contract and the
  Read ticket contract: no observation or target registration from a
  read result before G5 revalidation inside the delivery lock.
- **Exclusive answer paths** (spec §5.1). A held request has
  `answer_path: hook`; it switches to `keys` only after its hold ended,
  inside the delivery lock. No target exists while its hold is open.
- **Keys targets** (P1 follow-ups). Built only with
  `KeysTarget::from_screen` from a read for this occurrence, bound to the
  pane incarnation (`for_incarnation`, Task 6), registered only after
  `core.agent(agent_id)` is `Some` and the request is still `open`, with
  `request_id = core.request_id_for(agent_id, &key)`. Option keys are
  `"1"`..`"9"` or `"esc"`. Targets are set once per request id (Task 4).
- **Correlation keys** (spec §5.1 amended by D2): Claude
  `claude:<session_id>:<prompt_id>:<tool digest>`, Codex
  `codex:<session_id>:<turn_id>:<tool digest>`, both reusable. Tool
  digest: lowercase hex `sha256(tool_name ‖ 0x00 ‖
  canonical_json(tool_input))`. Occurrences follow the Occurrence
  contract, not the key alone.
- **Attribution** (spec §5.1, §6.2): `inbox` only when acd delivered and
  the correlated outcome is consistent; `terminal` when it contradicts or
  the dialog ended without acd; `uncertain` when acd delivered and the
  evidence is missing within 30 s, or a read needed to decide is
  unavailable (Task 15). O5 limits which events count.
- **Display text.** Strings from payloads or the registry go through
  `text::clean_display` (titles, labels, 200 chars) or
  `text::clean_screen` (detail, 4000 chars). `last_message` is capped at
  500 chars. The `allow_always` rule text is shown in full or the option
  is not offered (Task 7).
- **Logs.** Never log a token or a header value. Payloads only at
  `debug`, through `text::redact_log` (4 KiB). Rejected requests are
  logged at `warn` at most once a minute per remote, where the remote is
  an opaque id (the first 8 hex digits of `sha256` of the pane hint), and
  the rejection table holds at most 256 entries.
- **Files holding the hook token** (spec §6.1). Claude user settings only
  (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/settings.json` or `~/.claude/settings.json`).
  Refuse symlinks and foreign owners. One acd writer at a time
  (`<config_dir>/hooks.lock`). Detect intervening changes before the
  rename. Journal settings and record updates. Write atomically at 0600.
  Back up to `<config_dir>/backups/` (0700/0600). Refuse a Git-tracked
  file unless `--allow-tracked`, and an unknown Git status unless
  `--allow-unknown-git`; recheck before every token-bearing write.
  `hook-tokens.json`, `hooks-installed.json` and `hooks-journal.json` are
  0600 in the 0700 config dir, bounded by F1; preflight checks them.
- **Locks.** No `std::sync` lock is held across an `.await` or a `Core`
  call. The per-agent delivery lock (tokio) is the only lock held across
  awaits, and only around revalidation, observation and target
  registration; holders follow A7.
  Lock order: a delivery lock is never taken while `hooks.lock` is held
  and vice versa: the daemon takes `hooks.lock` only inside the
  `spawn_blocking` retirement call, which takes no delivery lock, and
  the CLI takes no delivery locks. `hooks.lock` follows T6.

## Review Focus

The inputs most likely to hurt a real user that no happy path covers.
Each has pinned tests in the task named.

1. **A screen that is not the reviewed command.** A dialog for a longer,
   shorter, quoted-whitespace-different or forged-description variant of
   the command, or a question with the same labels and different text,
   must never get a keys target. Tests: Task 13
   (`prefix_and_suffix_commands_do_not_match`,
   `quoted_whitespace_is_compared_exactly`,
   `a_forged_description_makes_the_match_ambiguous`,
   `same_labels_different_question_does_not_match`,
   `wrapped_rows_never_match`).
2. **A read that comes back too late, or never starts.** A capture or
   binding read that completes after `Stop`, a newer prompt, a T3
   takeover, a rebinding or a pane replacement, or after a newer read of
   the same purpose, must not open or enable anything; a read that found
   no slot must not invalidate another purpose's read. Tests: Task 15
   (`a_capture_after_stop_is_dropped`, `out_of_order_reads_keep_the_latest`,
   `a_capture_for_a_replaced_pane_is_dropped`,
   `a_read_that_is_not_admitted_does_not_bump_the_sequence`,
   `a_read_that_waits_for_a_slot_still_switches_to_keys`) and Task 17
   (`a_codex_watch_after_t3_takeover_opens_nothing`).
3. **An ambiguous Codex binding.** A rival pane that cannot be read,
   shows the same command, or appears during the read keeps the request
   read-only, also when a collapsed dialog is enlarged later; a live
   target is revoked when its session moves, its cwd becomes shared or a
   new rival shows the command. Tests: Task 17
   (`a_truncated_rival_keeps_the_request_from_opening`,
   `a_rival_added_during_the_bind_read_is_read_before_anything_opens`,
   `an_enlarged_dialog_with_a_rival_showing_the_command_gets_no_target`),
   Task 18 (`moving_the_codex_session_to_another_pane_revokes_the_live_target`,
   `a_second_codex_pane_in_the_cwd_revokes_an_only_in_cwd_target`,
   `a_new_rival_showing_the_command_revokes_the_live_target`,
   `a_live_target_sends_nothing_while_its_rival_recheck_is_blocked`,
   `a_live_target_sends_nothing_while_no_read_slot_is_free`,
   `the_lease_refuses_before_the_reconciler_notices_a_rival`).
4. **T3 takes over a session with live requests.** Bound Claude holds,
   Claude keys requests and Codex requests are revoked; the herdr agent
   stays. Tests: Task 18.
5. **Keys while the screen changes.** A dialog that changes while the
   engine waits for the delivery lock gets zero keys; a target for an old
   pane incarnation is never used. Tests: Task 4
   (`a_dialog_change_while_waiting_for_the_lock_sends_nothing`), Task 6
   (`a_target_for_an_old_incarnation_is_refused`).
6. **A held prompt answered "Yes" in the terminal; repeated commands.**
   A delayed outcome of an older identical request never resolves the
   newer one. Tests: Task 14
   (`terminal_allow_resolves_terminal_and_releases_the_hold`,
   `an_identical_command_before_evidence_opens_a_new_occurrence`,
   `a_delayed_outcome_of_an_older_identical_request_does_not_resolve_the_newer_one`,
   `a_new_turn_clears_the_shadow`), Task 16
   (`a_shadowed_expectation_ignores_identity_evidence`,
   `a_repeated_question_with_changed_options_ignores_the_older_answer`,
   `another_questions_outcome_neither_resolves_nor_counts`).
7. **Retained work under load.** Disconnected holds, a stalled delivery
   lock and floods of Codex permissions keep queues, read tasks and
   tracks bounded, and takeovers are still served. Tests: Task 8
   (`a_disconnected_hold_keeps_its_permit_until_its_messages_are_consumed`,
   `churning_disconnected_holds_never_grows_the_control_queue`), Task 19
   (all of `hooks_bounds.rs`, including the unique-session flood,
   evicted ambiguity and the real stalled keys holder in its read and
   write stages).
8. **Hook token rotation under load and failure.** A session that used
   the old token before rotation keeps it working; retirement needs a
   complete scan that started after the header update, measured after the
   lock; a crash between store and header writes is repairable; a failed
   retirement is reported; no path reacquires `hooks.lock`. Tests: Task
   21 (`a_failed_store_write_leaves_the_old_store_intact`,
   `ensure_on_a_legacy_only_dir_does_not_reacquire_the_lock`,
   `concurrent_migrations_agree`,
   `init_migrates_a_legacy_only_dir_without_hanging`), Task 26
   (`retention_excludes_only_sessions_seen_on_the_new_generation`,
   `retirement_needs_done_headers_and_a_complete_scan_that_started_after_them`,
   `freshness_is_measured_after_the_lock_is_held`,
   `retirement_does_nothing_when_any_field_of_the_store_changed`,
   `the_30_day_cap_always_wins`), Task 27
   (`a_crash_after_the_store_commit_is_repaired_by_install`,
   `header_completion_is_recorded_after_the_last_write`,
   `a_symlinked_settings_file_is_refused_before_committing`,
   `a_second_rotation_needs_force_while_the_previous_token_is_retained`,
   `rotation_refuses_a_tracked_file_before_committing`), Task 30
   (`the_daemon_and_init_migrate_a_legacy_dir_concurrently_without_hanging`),
   Task 31
   (`a_live_session_keeps_the_previous_token_until_it_is_seen_on_the_new_one`,
   `without_a_registry_scan_the_previous_token_stays`).
9. **Installer against a real settings file.** Concurrent edits, foreign
   entries mentioning the marker, Git tracked/untracked/unknown, changed
   config roots, record failures followed by Claude's own rewrite, an
   unreadable file during recovery, a batch that fails halfway. Tests:
   Tasks 23, 24, 25.

## Decisions

- **D1. One adapter for both agents.** `HooksAdapter` owns
  `AnswerPath::Hook` and `Source::Hooks`. Codex never uses the hook
  answer path and has no hooks-only agents.
- **D2. Claude keys include the tool digest and are reusable.**
  `prompt_id` is per user turn (fixtures share `e7cf77ea…`). The key
  `claude:<session>:<prompt_id>:<digest>` links the `AskUserQuestion`
  pair. Whether an event is a new occurrence is decided by the
  Occurrence contract, not by the key. Every end of a hook occurrence
  marks it gone (`DialogGoneById`), whatever the outcome, so the same key
  can open again. Amends spec §5.1.
- **D3. Codex evidence matches the command** (`PostToolUse.tool_input`
  drops `description`).
- **D4. No hooks-only Codex agents.** An unbound Codex request opens
  nothing; the herdr fallback shows it read-only.
- **D5. Codex cwd** uses `foreground_cwd` when present and compares
  canonicalized paths.
- **D6. Migration is a core operation** (`Observation::AgentMigrated`).
- **D7. Per-agent delivery lock.** The keys engine takes it before its
  final guard read and holds it until the key write completes; P3 takes
  it around every revalidation, observation and target registration on a
  herdr agent (finding B5 moved the acquisition before the guard read).
- **D8. Keys targets are set once** per request id, and bound to a pane
  incarnation.
- **D9. Claude permission keys after the hold**: `allow` (`1`) and
  `deny` (the `No` option, else `esc`); `allow_always` dropped.
- **D10. Supersede while awaiting evidence** resolves `uncertain` (O1).
  No late-evidence monitoring is kept (Sol question 4): the promise is
  dropped; a debug log notes the supersede.
- **D11. Registry status vs hook state**: hooks win for 30 s; a scan
  never turns `done` into `idle`.
- **D12. Hook token store** with generations (Contracts T1 to T5);
  replaces "one previous token" bookkeeping across several files.
- **D13. Hook listener bind failure is fatal.**
- **D14. Panic exits the daemon with 70.**
- **D15. Supervision**: SIGTERM/SIGINT exit 0; dead server 1; Tailscale
  change 75; launchd `KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit = false`, systemd
  `Restart=on-failure`.
- **D16. Settings edits preserve bytes.** Top-level object, the `hooks`
  object, each group and each group's `hooks` array are parsed as
  ordered raw values; foreign keys and foreign entries keep their exact
  bytes, including inside a group that also held an owned entry.
- **D17. The shim forwards `HERDR_*` hints**; acd never binds Codex with
  them.
- **D18. `last_message` only for hooks-only agents.**
- **D19. herdr `state_change_seq` stays log-only.**
- **D20. `find_dialog` stays fail-closed** on a status line below the
  footer.
- **D21. T3 decode-error degradation already clears** (P4).
- **D22. Exact ownership markers.** An entry is owned only when it
  parses exactly as acd's entry for that event (Task 23). An entry that
  carries the marker but does not parse is "suspicious": install and
  uninstall leave it and warn; rotation refuses to write any token while
  one exists.
- **D23. Git status is tracked, untracked or unknown**; unknown refuses
  without `--allow-unknown-git`.
- **D24. Installer records are keyed by canonical destination**, so a
  changed `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` creates a new record and rotation and the
  registry scan visit every recorded root (Sol question 3).
- **D25. Malformed `AskUserQuestion` input** (any question or option
  failing structural validation) is shown read-only (Sol question 2,
  Task 7).
- **D26. Wrapped or multi-line commands get no screen binding and no
  keys target** (M4). A held Claude request is still answerable through
  its HTTP hold; an unheld or Codex one is read-only.

## Obligation map (P1 follow-ups, "P3: hooks and service")

| Obligation | Where |
|---|---|
| Answer revisions: keys target immutable per occurrence | Task 4 (set-once), Task 6 (incarnation), D8 |
| Ownership rule 1 via `SessionMap`, re-evaluate on `changes()` | Tasks 10, 11, 18 |
| `project.removed` inferred field unconfirmed | Out of scope |
| T3 decode-error degradation clears after 10 min | D21 |
| Reuse `text::redact_log` for hook payload logging | Task 7 |

Two chains share no files: the core/herdr/hooks chain (2 to 19) and the
`acd` binary chain (1, 20 to 29). They meet in Task 30. Tasks 7, 8, 9,
12 and 13 each add one `pub mod` line to
`crates/acd-adapters/src/hooks/mod.rs`; that one-line merge conflict is
expected and the integration owner resolves it. Task 7 adds every new
`acd-adapters` dependency. Every `crates/acd/src/main.rs` edit is in the
binary chain, which runs one task at a time. Integration owner: the
orchestrator merges task branches into `p3-hooks` in table order and
runs the full workspace checks after each merge. A task that finds it
must edit a file owned by a task running in parallel stops and reports.

Sizing: Tasks 7, 11, 15, 17 and 24 are the largest (mostly test and
code listings). If one runs well past 20 minutes, split it at its step
boundary without renumbering: 15 after Step 3 (tickets and `ReadSlots`
with their unit tests) and 17 after the pure validation pieces
(`pane_seen`, `codex_decision` and the inventory) before the reconciler
wiring.

### Task 1: Test harness: free ports with retry, environment isolation

Fixes the unlogged CI flake (macOS, 2026-10-09): `free_port()` in
`crates/acd/tests/cli.rs` binds port 0, drops the listener and returns
the port, and a parallel test can take that port before the daemon binds
it. The daemon then exits with `cannot bind`, and `status_when_ready`
fails after 10 s. P3 adds a second listener (`hook_port`), which doubles
the exposure, so the fix covers both ports and lives in a shared module.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs`
- Modify: `crates/acd/tests/cli.rs` (use `common`; delete its `acd`,
  `free_port`, `Daemon`, `init_loopback`)
- Modify: `crates/acd/tests/daemon_tls.rs` (use `common`; delete its
  `acd`, `Daemon`, `init`, `start`)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (later `crates/acd/tests/*.rs` files use these exact names):
  - `common::acd(dir: &Path) -> Command`: sets `ACD_CONFIG_DIR=dir`,
    `HOME=dir`, removes `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`,
    `CODEX_HOME`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `RUST_LOG` and every
    `ACD_TEST_*` override.
  - Readiness: `start_daemon` returns only after an authenticated
    `acd status` succeeds (Sol minor 2), never on socket existence.
  - `common::acd_home(dir: &Path, home: &Path) -> Command`: the same,
    with a separate `HOME`.
  - `common::free_port() -> u16`.
  - `common::Daemon(pub Child)`: killed and reaped on drop.
  - `common::Started { pub daemon: Daemon, pub port: u16, pub hook_port: u16, pub log: PathBuf }`.
  - `common::start_daemon(dir: &Path, extra_config: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Started`.
  - `common::start_daemon_with(dir, extra_config, env, pick: impl FnMut() -> (u16, u16)) -> Started`.
  - `common::init_dir(parent: &Path) -> PathBuf`: runs `acd init` in
    `parent/acd` and returns it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs` with only the test first, at the
bottom of a new `crates/acd/tests/harness.rs`:

```rust
mod common;

#[test]
fn start_retries_when_a_port_is_taken_before_the_daemon_binds() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    let blocker = std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
    let taken = blocker.local_addr().unwrap().port();
    let mut picks = vec![(taken, common::free_port()), (common::free_port(), common::free_port())].into_iter();
    let started = common::start_daemon_with(&dir, "", &[], || picks.next().expect("at most two attempts"));
    assert_ne!(started.port, taken, "the second attempt must be used");
    assert!(acd(&dir).arg("status").status().unwrap().success(), "ready means status works");
}

#[test]
fn a_stale_socket_is_not_readiness() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    // A socket file left by a crashed daemon, with nothing behind it.
    drop(std::os::unix::net::UnixListener::bind(dir.join("acd.sock")).unwrap());
    let started = common::start_daemon(&dir, "", &[]);
    assert!(common::acd(&dir).arg("status").status().unwrap().success());
    drop(started);
}

#[test]
fn every_command_is_isolated_from_the_user_environment() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let cmd = common::acd(tmp.path());
    let envs: Vec<_> = cmd.get_envs().map(|(k, v)| (k.to_owned(), v.map(|v| v.to_owned()))).collect();
    for k in common::ISOLATED {
        assert!(envs.iter().any(|(n, v)| n == k && v.is_none()), "{k} must be removed");
    }
    assert!(envs.iter().any(|(n, v)| n == "HOME" && v.as_deref() == Some(tmp.path().as_os_str())));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the shared module**

`crates/acd/tests/common/mod.rs`:

```rust
//! Shared helpers for tests that run the `acd` binary. Every command is
//! isolated from the developer's environment: HOME and ACD_CONFIG_DIR
//! point into a temp dir, and variables that would reach real herdr,
//! Claude or Codex state are removed.
#![allow(dead_code)]
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Child, Command, Stdio};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

pub const ISOLATED: [&str; 15] = [
    "HERDR_SOCKET_PATH",
    "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR",
    "CODEX_HOME",
    "CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT",
    "RUST_LOG",
    // Debug-only test overrides must never leak in from the outer shell.
    "ACD_TEST_MAX_UNAUTH",
    "ACD_TEST_SERVICE_RUNNER",
    "ACD_TEST_PANIC_AFTER_MS",
    "ACD_TEST_STOP_SERVER",
    "ACD_TEST_FAIL_AT",
    "ACD_TEST_HOOKS_FAST",
    "ACD_TEST_GIT",
    "ACD_TEST_TOUCH_SETTINGS",
    "ACD_TEST_SERVICE_FAIL",
    "ACD_TEST_PAUSE_AT",
];

pub fn acd(dir: &Path) -> Command {
    acd_home(dir, dir)
}

pub fn acd_home(dir: &Path, home: &Path) -> Command {
    let mut c = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_acd"));
    c.env("ACD_CONFIG_DIR", dir).env("HOME", home);
    for k in ISOLATED {
        c.env_remove(k);
    }
    c
}

pub fn free_port() -> u16 {
    std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
        .unwrap()
        .local_addr()
        .unwrap()
        .port()
}

/// A running `acd run`, killed when dropped so a failed assert cannot
/// leave it behind.
pub struct Daemon(pub Child);

impl Drop for Daemon {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.0.kill().ok();
        self.0.wait().ok();
    }
}

pub struct Started {
    pub daemon: Daemon,
    pub port: u16,
    pub hook_port: u16,
    pub log: PathBuf,
}

pub fn init_dir(parent: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    let dir = parent.join("acd");
    let out = acd(&dir).arg("init").output().unwrap();
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
    dir
}

/// Writes `config.toml` with fresh ports and starts `acd run`, retrying
/// the whole start when the daemon could not bind (another test took the
/// port between `free_port` and the bind).
pub fn start_daemon(dir: &Path, extra_config: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Started {
    start_daemon_with(dir, extra_config, env, || (free_port(), free_port()))
}

pub fn start_daemon_with(
    dir: &Path,
    extra_config: &str,
    env: &[(&str, &str)],
    mut pick: impl FnMut() -> (u16, u16),
) -> Started {
    for attempt in 0..5 {
        let (port, hook_port) = pick();
        // A caller that sets `listen` itself gets no default (TOML forbids
        // duplicate keys).
        let listen = if extra_config.contains("listen =") { "" } else { "listen = [\"loopback\"]\n" };
        // Overwriting keeps the 0600 mode `acd init` gave the file.
        std::fs::write(
            dir.join("config.toml"),
            format!("port = {port}\nhook_port = {hook_port}\n{listen}{extra_config}"),
        )
        .unwrap();
        let log = dir.join(format!("daemon-{attempt}.log"));
        let mut cmd = acd(dir);
        for (k, v) in env {
            cmd.env(k, v);
        }
        let child = cmd
            .arg("run")
            .stdout(Stdio::null())
            .stderr(std::fs::File::create(&log).unwrap())
            .spawn()
            .unwrap();
        let mut daemon = Daemon(child);
        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
        loop {
            // Ready means an authenticated `acd status` over this daemon's
            // socket succeeds: a stale socket from an earlier run fails it,
            // and from Task 30 on the daemon binds the hook listener before
            // it binds `acd.sock`, so a ready daemon also serves hooks.
            if ready(dir) {
                return Started { daemon, port, hook_port, log };
            }
            if let Some(status) = daemon.0.try_wait().unwrap() {
                let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&log).unwrap_or_default();
                if text.contains("cannot bind") || text.contains("Address already in use") {
                    break; // retry with new ports
                }
                panic!("acd run exited with {status}: {text}");
            }
            assert!(Instant::now() < deadline, "daemon did not start");
            std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20));
        }
    }
    panic!("could not start acd after 5 attempts");
}

fn ready(dir: &Path) -> bool {
    dir.join("acd.sock").exists()
        && acd(dir).arg("status").stdout(Stdio::null()).stderr(Stdio::null()).status().is_ok_and(|s| s.success())
}
```

`hook_port` is ignored by `Config` until Task 30 (unknown keys are
ignored), so the helper is correct before and after that task.

- [ ] **Step 4: Move `cli.rs` and `daemon_tls.rs` onto the module**

In `crates/acd/tests/cli.rs`:

- Add `mod common;` and `use common::{acd, free_port, Daemon};` at the
  top. Delete the local `acd`, `free_port` and `Daemon`.
- Replace `init_loopback(name)` with a version that only initializes and
  sets the machine name, and start daemons through `start_daemon`:

```rust
/// An initialized config dir; `start` writes ports and starts the daemon.
fn init_named(name: &str) -> (tempfile::TempDir, std::path::PathBuf) {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    (tmp, dir)
}

fn start(dir: &std::path::Path, name: &str, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> common::Started {
    common::start_daemon(dir, &format!("machine_name = \"{name}\"\n"), env)
}
```

- `run_serves_loopback_and_status_reports_machine`: replace
  `init_loopback("e2e-box")` and `Daemon(acd(&dir).arg("run").spawn())`
  with `init_named` and `let _d = start(&dir, "e2e-box", &[]);`. Keep
  the stale-socket line before `start`.
- `trace_logs_never_contain_the_token`: use
  `let started = start(&dir, "log-box", &[("RUST_LOG", rust_log)]);` and
  read `started.log` instead of the hand-made log file. Drop the daemon
  (`drop(started)`) before reading the log.
- `second_daemon_on_another_port_leaves_the_live_socket_alone`: start
  the first with `start`; the second keeps `run_expecting_exit` with a
  config written by hand (`port = {free_port()}`, `hook_port =
  {free_port()}`). A second daemon that loses the port race also exits,
  and the assertion accepts either message:

```rust
assert!(err.contains("another acd is running") || err.contains("cannot bind"), "{err}");
```

  then re-run `run_expecting_exit` once if the message was `cannot
  bind`, and require `another acd is running` on that run.
- Tests that call `run_expecting_exit` and expect an early failure
  (`run_refuses_empty_or_short_client_token`,
  `run_fails_when_a_listen_address_cannot_be_bound`) keep their
  hand-written configs; they never reach a bind or bind a port held by
  the test itself.

In `crates/acd/tests/daemon_tls.rs`: add `mod common;`, delete `acd`,
`Daemon`, `init` and `start`, and replace them with:

```rust
/// Callers that passed `listen` to the old `init` now pass it to `start`.
fn init() -> (tempfile::TempDir, std::path::PathBuf) {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let dir = common::init_dir(tmp.path());
    (tmp, dir)
}

fn start(dir: &std::path::Path, listen: &str, debug_log: bool, admission: Option<usize>) -> common::Started {
    let mut env: Vec<(&str, String)> = Vec::new();
    if debug_log { env.push(("RUST_LOG", "debug".into())); }
    if let Some(n) = admission { env.push(("ACD_TEST_MAX_UNAUTH", n.to_string())); }
    let env: Vec<(&str, &str)> = env.iter().map(|(k, v)| (*k, v.as_str())).collect();
    common::start_daemon(dir, &format!("listen = {listen}\n"), &env)
}
```

The helper omits its default `listen` line because `extra_config` sets
one. Callers read `started.port` where they used the returned port, and
`started.log` where they passed a log path.

### Task 2: Core: agent migration and pending-key lookup

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/acd-core/src/registry.rs`
- Modify: `crates/acd-core/src/engine.rs`
- Create: `crates/acd-core/tests/migrate.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `Observation::AgentMigrated { from: String, to: String }`.
  - `Registry::pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)>`:
    the agent's pending request id, its primary key and kind.
  - `Core::pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)>`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`crates/acd-core/tests/migrate.rs`:

```rust
use acd_core::testing::{agent_obs, permission_obs};
use acd_core::*;
use acd_protocol::*;

fn hooks_agent(id: &str) -> AgentObs {
    AgentObs { source: Source::Hooks, can_send: false, ..agent_obs(id) }
}

fn reg() -> Registry {
    let mut r = Registry::new("m".into());
    r.apply(Observation::AgentUpsert(hooks_agent("claude:s1")));
    r.apply(Observation::AgentUpsert(agent_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1")));
    r
}

#[test]
fn the_open_request_moves_and_keeps_its_id() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let rid = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(matches!(&changes[0], Change::AgentUpdated(a) if a.id == "herdr:h:w1:p1"
        && a.pending.as_ref().map(|p| p.id.as_str()) == Some(rid.as_str())
        && a.state == AgentState::NeedsYou));
    assert_eq!(changes.last(), Some(&Change::AgentRemoved("claude:s1".into())));
    assert!(!changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { .. } | Change::RequestOpened(_))));
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").is_none());
    assert_eq!(r.request(&rid).unwrap().agent_id, "herdr:h:w1:p1");
    // The key moved too: a replay on the new agent updates in place.
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "k")));
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, rid);
    let ended = r.apply(Observation::RequestEnded {
        agent_id: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into(), key: CorrelationKey("k".into()), by: ResolvedBy::Terminal,
    });
    assert!(ended.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, .. } if *request_id == rid)));
}

#[test]
fn a_claimed_request_moves_with_its_claim() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let rid = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.claim(&rid, Answer::Option("allow".into()), None).unwrap();
    r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert_eq!(r.request(&rid).unwrap().status, RequestStatus::Answering);
    let done = r.apply(Observation::RequestEndedById { request_id: rid.clone(), by: ResolvedBy::Inbox });
    assert!(done.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { option_id: Some(o), .. } if o == "allow")));
}

#[test]
fn a_fallback_on_the_target_is_superseded() {
    let mut r = reg();
    let mut fb = permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "herdr:h:w1:p1:fp");
    fb.kind = RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed;
    fb.answer_path = AnswerPath::None;
    r.apply(Observation::FallbackSeen(fb));
    let fb_id = r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, by: ResolvedBy::Superseded, .. } if *request_id == fb_id)));
    assert_ne!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, fb_id);
}

#[test]
fn a_structured_request_on_the_target_wins() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "other")));
    let kept = r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    let moving = r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id.clone();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert!(changes.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Change::RequestResolved { request_id, by: ResolvedBy::Lost, .. } if *request_id == moving)));
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, kept);
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").is_none());
}

#[test]
fn migration_without_a_request_only_removes_the_source() {
    let mut r = reg();
    let changes = r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:w1:p1".into() });
    assert_eq!(changes, vec![Change::AgentRemoved("claude:s1".into())]);
}

#[test]
fn migration_to_an_unknown_agent_is_a_no_op() {
    let mut r = reg();
    r.apply(Observation::RequestSeen(permission_obs("claude:s1", "k")));
    assert!(r.apply(Observation::AgentMigrated { from: "claude:s1".into(), to: "herdr:h:nope".into() }).is_empty());
    assert!(r.agent("claude:s1").unwrap().pending.is_some());
}

#[test]
fn pending_key_names_the_primary_key() {
    let mut r = reg();
    assert!(r.pending_key("herdr:h:w1:p1").is_none());
    let mut fb = permission_obs("herdr:h:w1:p1", "herdr:h:w1:p1:fp");
    fb.kind = RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed;
    fb.answer_path = AnswerPath::None;
    r.apply(Observation::FallbackSeen(fb));
    let (rid, key, kind) = r.pending_key("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(key, CorrelationKey("herdr:h:w1:p1:fp".into()));
    assert_eq!(kind, RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed);
    assert_eq!(r.agent("herdr:h:w1:p1").unwrap().pending.as_ref().unwrap().id, rid);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `registry.rs`, add the variant to `Observation`:

```rust
    /// Moves `from`'s pending request (same id, status and revision) and
    /// its keys to `to`, then removes `from` (spec §6.2 Binding, D6).
    AgentMigrated {
        from: String,
        to: String,
    },
```

and the arm in `apply`:

```rust
            Observation::AgentMigrated { from, to } => self.migrate(&from, &to),
```

Add to `impl Registry`:

```rust
    pub fn pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)> {
        let p = self.agents.get(agent_id)?.pending.as_ref()?;
        let e = self.requests.get(&p.id)?;
        Some((p.id.clone(), e.key.clone(), e.req.kind))
    }

    fn migrate(&mut self, from: &str, to: &str) -> Vec<Change> {
        if from == to || !self.agents.contains_key(from) || !self.agents.contains_key(to) {
            return vec![];
        }
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let moving = self.agents.get(from).and_then(|a| a.pending.clone());
        if let Some(p) = moving {
            match self.agents.get(to).and_then(|a| a.pending.clone()) {
                Some(q) if q.kind == RequestKind::BlockedUnparsed && q.status == RequestStatus::Open => {
                    out.extend(
                        self.resolve_request(&q.id, ResolvedBy::Superseded)
                            .into_iter()
                            .filter(|c| !matches!(c, Change::AgentUpdated(_))),
                    );
                }
                Some(_) => {
                    let by = if p.status == RequestStatus::Answering {
                        ResolvedBy::Uncertain
                    } else {
                        ResolvedBy::Lost
                    };
                    out.extend(
                        self.resolve_request(&p.id, by)
                            .into_iter()
                            .filter(|c| !matches!(c, Change::AgentUpdated(_))),
                    );
                    out.extend(self.agent_gone(from));
                    return out;
                }
                None => {}
            }
            if let Some(e) = self.requests.get_mut(&p.id) {
                e.req.agent_id = to.to_string();
            }
            let moved: Vec<(String, CorrelationKey)> = self
                .keys
                .keys()
                .filter(|(a, _)| a == from)
                .cloned()
                .collect();
            for (a, k) in moved {
                let mut st = self.keys.remove(&(a, k.clone())).expect("listed above");
                st.agent_id = to.to_string();
                self.keys.entry((to.to_string(), k)).or_insert(st);
            }
            let req = self.requests.get(&p.id).map(|e| e.req.clone()).expect("moved request");
            self.seen.remove(to);
            let a = self.agents.get_mut(to).expect("checked above");
            a.pending = Some(req);
            if a.state != AgentState::NeedsYou {
                a.state = AgentState::NeedsYou;
                a.state_since = Utc::now();
            }
            out.push(Change::AgentUpdated(a.clone()));
        }
        // The request moved: nothing is resolved here.
        self.agents.remove(from);
        self.keys.retain(|_, k| k.agent_id != from);
        self.seen.remove(from);
        out.push(Change::AgentRemoved(from.to_string()));
        out
    }
```

In `engine.rs`, next to `request_id_for`:

```rust
    pub fn pending_key(&self, agent_id: &str) -> Option<(String, CorrelationKey, RequestKind)> {
        self.inner.lock().unwrap().registry.pending_key(agent_id)
    }
```

(`RequestKind` comes from `acd_protocol::*`, already imported.)

### Task 3: Resolution `reason` on `request.resolved`

P2's keys engine already resolves a stray keystroke `uncertain`; clients
cannot tell it from other uncertain outcomes. This task adds an optional
`reason` and maps `stray_keystroke` to "A stray keystroke may be in the
input box" (P1 follow-ups; spec §6.2 "After a keystroke").

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/acd-protocol/src/model.rs`, `src/messages.rs`,
  `PROTOCOL.md`, `schema/server.schema.json` (regenerated)
- Create: `crates/acd-protocol/examples/server/request.resolved.stray.json`
- Modify: `crates/acd-protocol/tests/messages.rs`, `tests/model.rs`
- Modify: `crates/acd-core/src/registry.rs`, `src/log.rs`,
  `tests/claims.rs`, `tests/registry.rs` (struct literals)
- Modify: `crates/acd-client/src/session.rs`, `src/conn.rs`,
  `src/inbox.rs`, `src/view.rs`
- Modify: `app/src/lib/bindings/*.ts` (regenerated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's registry.
- Produces:
  - `acd_protocol::ResolveReason { StrayKeystroke, Unknown }`
    (`#[serde(other)]` on `Unknown`), serialized `"stray_keystroke"`.
  - `ServerMessage::RequestResolved { …, reason: Option<ResolveReason> }`
    (`default`, `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"`).
  - `Change::RequestResolved { …, reason: Option<ResolveReason> }`.
  - `Observation::RequestEndedWithReason { request_id: String, by: ResolvedBy, reason: ResolveReason }`.
  - `Registry::resolve_request_with(&mut self, request_id: &str, by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Vec<Change>`;
    `resolve_request` calls it with `None`.
  - `acd_client::view::resolution_notice(by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Option<Notice>`.

- `view.rs`:

```rust
pub const NOTICE_STRAY_KEYSTROKE: &str = "A stray keystroke may be in the input box";

pub fn resolution_notice(by: ResolvedBy, reason: Option<ResolveReason>) -> Option<Notice> {
    if by == ResolvedBy::Uncertain && reason == Some(ResolveReason::StrayKeystroke) {
        return Some(Notice { tone: NoticeTone::Warning, text: NOTICE_STRAY_KEYSTROKE.into() });
    }
    match by {
        ResolvedBy::Terminal => Some(Notice { tone: NoticeTone::Info, text: "Answered in the terminal".into() }),
        ResolvedBy::Uncertain | ResolvedBy::Lost | ResolvedBy::Unknown => Some(Notice {
            tone: NoticeTone::Warning,
            text: NOTICE_CHECK_TERMINAL.into(),
        }),
        ResolvedBy::Inbox | ResolvedBy::Agent | ResolvedBy::Superseded => None,
    }
}
```

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Observation::RequestEndedWithReason`, `ResolveReason` (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - `KeysTargets::set(&self, t: KeysTarget) -> Result<(), &'static str>`:
    first registration wins; an identical re-set is `Ok`; a different
    target for a live or retired request id is `Err`.
  - `herdr::keys::AgentLocks` with
    `pub async fn lock(&self, agent_id: &str) -> tokio::sync::OwnedMutexGuard<()>`.
  - `HerdrAdapter::agent_locks(&self) -> Arc<AgentLocks>`.
  - The keys engine resolves a stray with
    `RequestEndedWithReason { by: Uncertain, reason: StrayKeystroke }`.

```rust
    #[test]
    fn a_target_is_set_once_per_request_id() {
        let (t, dialog) = claude_target();
        let targets = KeysTargets::default();
        targets.set(t.clone()).unwrap();
        targets.set(t.clone()).unwrap(); // identical: no-op
        let redrawn = dialog.replace("⏺ Creating empty file", "⏺ Creating an empty file");
        let other = KeysTarget::from_screen(
            "r1".into(), "a".into(), CorrelationKey("k".into()), &redrawn,
            [("allow".to_string(), "1".to_string())].into(),
        ).unwrap();
        assert!(targets.set(other.clone()).is_err(), "a different target never replaces");
        assert_eq!(targets.get("r1").unwrap().fingerprint(), t.fingerprint());
        targets.remove("r1");
        assert!(targets.set(other).is_err(), "a retired request id never gets a target again");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn agent_locks_serialize_per_agent_and_forget_idle_entries() {
        let locks = Arc::new(AgentLocks::default());
        let g = locks.lock("a").await;
        let l2 = locks.clone();
        let waiter = tokio::spawn(async move { l2.lock("a").await; });
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;
        assert!(!waiter.is_finished(), "a second lock on the same agent waits");
        let _other = locks.lock("b").await; // another agent does not wait
        drop(g);
        waiter.await.unwrap();
        drop(_other);
        let _ = locks.lock("c").await;
        assert!(locks.len() <= 1, "idle entries are pruned");
    }
```

In `crates/acd-adapters/tests/herdr_adapter.rs`, replace
